the publications page itself was not fetched, so titles/venues are unconfirmed.","VERSES AI's independent financial condition (revenue, cash runway, customer count) — only the company's own newsletter and press releases were consulted; no independent financial source was fetched. The AXIOM 'beats DreamerV3' result has a vendor-commissioned audit (Soothsayer Analytics) but no independent replication was found in this session.","Current ACT-R software version number — the project homepage does not display it (copyright notice shows 2002-2013, likely a stale template); 'ACT-R 7' as latest version comes from undated secondary sources.","Maturity/peer-review status of the 2026 'Theater of Mind' GWA preprint (arXiv 2604.08206) — exists and was fetched, but no citations, venue acceptance, or independent implementations were found; treat as unvalidated single-author work.","Exact license of Microsoft GraphRAG, V*/SEAL code, ZoomEye, and CropVLM repositories — not checked in this session; verify on each repo before any code reuse.","The claim that JEPA (LeCun's architecture) is 'one of the two most influential cognitive architecture frameworks for AI agents' — asserted by arXiv 2604.11364 and search summaries; JEPA's standing relative to CoALA was not independently verified in this session."]},"verdicts":[{"claim":"IBM sold the data/analytics assets of Watson Health to Francisco Partners for an estimated $1B in 2022; the assets were rebranded as standalone company Merative, and coverage frames the reorganization as an explicit move away from the 'cognitive computing' moniker toward pragmatic data analytics.","verdict":"confirmed","evidence":"Independently corroborated by multiple non-original sources: IBM's own newsroom (2022-01-21, 'Francisco Partners to Acquire IBM's Healthcare Data and Analytics Assets'), Francisco Partners' press release (completion 2022-06-30, launch of standalone company Merative, HQ Ann Arbor), HealthExec ('Francisco Partners completes $1B buyout of IBM Watson Health' — price was reported/estimated at ~$1B, never officially disclosed by IBM), Wikipedia (Merative), Fierce Healthcare, HCI Innovation Group, and Becker's. The framing component also checks out independently: coverage (healthcare.digital, StatNews follow-up 2022-11-21) describes the reorganization into six pragmatic data product families (Health Insights, MarketScan, Micromedex, etc.) as a move away from the cognitive-computing/AI-moonshot positioning, with Merative's CEO quoted saying 'you will not see us investing directly to build major AI models' — the value is the health data, not the AI engine. Key URLs: https://newsroom.ibm.com/2022-01-21-Francisco-Partners-to-Acquire-IBMs-Healthcare-Data-and-Analytics-Assets · https://www.franciscopartners.com/media/Merative · https://healthexec.com/topics/healthcare-management/mergers-and-acquisitions/francisco-partners-completes-1b-buyout-ibm · https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/health-tech/watson-health-under-new-investment-firm-ownership-reborn-merative"},{"claim":"IBM's current enterprise AI brand is watsonx, unveiled at Think on 2023-05-09 as an 'AI and data platform' (watsonx.ai / .data / .governance) — marketed as AI for business, with no 'cognitive computing' framing.","verdict":"confirmed","evidence":"Multiple independent sources beyond the cited IBM press release confirm: Wikipedia (IBM Watsonx), TechTarget news ('IBM launches Watsonx, a new generative AI platform'), TechChannel, and IBM's separate announcement page 'Introducing watsonx: The future of AI for business' all date the unveiling to the Think conference on May 9, 2023, describe it as an 'AI and data platform' with exactly the three components watsonx.ai (foundation-model studio, GA July 2023), watsonx.data (open lakehouse data store), and watsonx.governance (AI governance toolkit). All coverage frames it in foundation-model/generative-AI/'AI for business' vocabulary; none of the independent coverage uses 'cognitive computing' framing, consistent with the deprecation thesis. Key URLs: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM_Watsonx · https://www.techtarget.com/searchenterpriseai/news/366537216/IBM-launches-Watsonx-a-new-generative-AI-platform · https://www.ibm.com/new/announcements/introducing-watsonx-the-future-of-ai-for-business"},{"claim":"The term 'cognitive computing' still exists as a defined educational category (TechTarget maintains a current definition closely associating it with IBM Watson, distinguishing it from AI as 'assisting human decision-making'), so it is dormant-definitional rather than formally retired.","verdict":"confirmed","evidence":"Live fetch of https://www.techtarget.com/searchenterpriseai/definition/cognitive-computing on 2026-06-11 confirms the page is current (published Jun 29, 2023, no retirement notice). It defines cognitive computing as 'the use of computerized models to simulate the human thought process in complex situations where the answers might be ambiguous and uncertain', states 'The phrase is closely associated with IBM's cognitive computer system, Watson', and draws the AI distinction exactly as claimed: 'cognitive computing is used to assist humans in decision-making processes' while 'AI relies on algorithms to solve a problem'. This is a claim about the content of a specific page, so direct inspection of the live page is the appropriate verification; the page exists, says what the claim says, and shows no sign of formal retirement — supporting the 'dormant-definitional' characterization."},{"claim":"Gartner's 2025 AI Hype Cycle places AI agents (and AI-ready data) as the fastest-advancing technologies at the Peak of Inflated Expectations; Gartner also predicts >40% of agentic AI projects will be canceled by end-2027 and coined 'agent washing' for rebranding non-agentic products.","verdict":"confirmed","evidence":"Independent (non-Gartner) coverage corroborates every element. Hype Cycle: BusinessWire syndication (2025-08-08) and third-party analyses (testRigor, BizTechReports) confirm 'AI agents and AI-ready data are the two fastest advancing technologies' on the 2025 Gartner Hype Cycle for AI, both at the Peak of Inflated Expectations. Cancellation prediction: MarTech, Search Engine Land, and BigDATAwire/HPCwire all independently report Gartner's June 25, 2025 prediction that over 40% of agentic AI projects will be canceled by end of 2027 (escalating costs, unclear business value, inadequate risk controls), and quote the 'agent washing' term — vendors rebranding AI assistants, RPA and chatbots without substantial agentic capabilities, with Gartner estimating only ~130 of thousands of agentic AI vendors are real. One pedantic note: whether Gartner literally 'coined' agent washing is hard to prove, but the term is universally attributed to Gartner in coverage. Key URLs: https://www.businesswire.com/news/home/20250808070139/en/Gartner-Hype-Cycle-Identifies-Top-AI-Innovations-in-2025 · https://martech.org/gartner-40-of-agentic-ai-projects-will-fail-making-humans-indispensable/ · https://www.hpcwire.com/bigdatawire/this-just-in/gartner-predicts-over-40-of-agentic-ai-projects-will-be-canceled-by-end-of-2027/"},{"claim":"ACT-R remains an active academic cognitive-modeling project at CMU: the project homepage announces a 2026 ACT-R Workshop and lists recently uploaded publications (EEG, memory prediction, cognitive modeling), though no current software version number is displayed.","verdict":"confirmed","evidence":"Two-pronged verification. (1) Direct re-fetch of http://act-r.psy.cmu.edu/ on 2026-06-11 (this is a claim about page content, so direct inspection is the verification): the Announcements section links a '2026 ACT-R Workshop'; recent publications listed include EEG ('Combining EEG signals from the 2 members of a team to improve event identification'), memory prediction ('Using the Environment to Predict Memory Performance'; 'Individual Differences in Long-Term Forgetting: Evidence for Storage Degradation'), and cognitive modeling ('Understanding the complexity of music improvisation: Leveraging cognitive models to inform adaptive instruction design'); no software version number appears on the homepage. (2) Independent corroboration of activity: web search confirms a Spring 2026 Special Workshop on the Futures of Cognitive Architectures and the ACT-R Ecosystem, a 23rd Annual Post-Graduate Summer School, ACT-R inclusion in the Groningen Spring School in Cognitive Modeling (March 30–April 2, 2026), and CMU institutional pages (HCII) listing ACT-R as an active project led by John Anderson and Christian Lebiere. Key URLs: https://act-r.psy.cmu.edu/ · https://www.ai.rug.nl/springschool/the-models/act-r/ · https://hcii.cmu.edu/project/act-r"},{"claim":"Soar is actively maintained open source: current release 9.6.5 (chunking test-literalization operator, CMake/SWIG migration), recent bug fixes to episodic memory (EpMem) and semantic memory (SMem), a new ROS2 integration package (soar_ros), and an 'Engineer's Guide to Soar' course; maintained by SoarGroup on GitHub.","verdict":"confirmed","evidence":"Verified against sources independent of the cited soar.eecs.umich.edu homepage. GitHub releases page (https://github.com/SoarGroup/Soar/releases — maintained under the SoarGroup org): latest release Soar 9.6.5, May 6, 2026, with release notes confirming 'new chunking literalization control via the $ test' (the claimed test-literalization operator), CMake-based build now equal to SCons, Conan-managed dependencies with built-in SWIG bindings (Python/Java/Tcl/JavaScript/C#), 'a meaningful round of chunking, episodic-memory, and justification bug fixes', and 'a quieter SMem trace' (plus SMem LTI aliases in 9.6.4) — matching the EpMem/SMem fix claims. soar_ros: independently confirmed as a real ROS 2 package on the official ROS index (https://index.ros.org/p/soar_ros/), docs.ros.org, GitHub (THA-Embedded-Systems-Lab/soar_ros), and Open Robotics Discourse — minor nuance: it is developed by the Technical University of Applied Sciences Augsburg, not by SoarGroup itself, though it is featured as a new integration. 'Engineer's Guide to Soar': independently confirmed as a 14-part course on programming Soar created by Bryan Stearns, available on GitHub. All elements of the claim hold."}]},{"key":"assistive-technology","findings":{"summary":"Assistive technology (AT) for blind/low-vision (BLV) users provides the strongest single demand proof for AI vision-description services that exists today, and it is now backed by binding law on three continents. DEMAND: Be My Eyes alone reached 1 million BLV users and 10 million volunteers in March 2026 (verified on bemyeyes.com), serving 43M+ AI/video requests per year across 150+ countries and 180+ languages; its Be My AI feature launched on OpenAI GPT-4 in 2023 and is free to end users while enterprise contracts (Microsoft, Hilton, Google, Spotify) fund it — revenue reportedly growing 300%/yr. Microsoft Seeing AI (free, iOS+Android, 18+ languages) added GPT-style rich scene descriptions and document chat. Aira proves willingness-to-pay: human visual interpreting costs $26–$1,160/month (20–880 min) while its AI tier (Access AI) is free with optional human verification of AI answers — a hybrid pattern directly relevant to MCP design. Hardware spans $399 (Envision Ally Solos pre-order) to ~$4,250 (OrCam MyEye 3 Pro, offline). Google Lookout remains active with Gemini-powered image Q&A. WHO counts 2.2 billion people with vision impairment (fact sheet updated Feb 2026). LAW: WCAG 2.2 SC 1.1.1 (Level A) requires text alternatives for all non-text content; the European Accessibility Act has been enforceable since 28 June 2025 for e-commerce, banking, transport, telecom and consumer devices — including non-EU companies selling into the EU (microenterprise exemption covers services only; existing services have until 2030); French advocacy groups served legal notices on retailers within days. BRAZIL: LBI (Lei 13.146/2015) Art. 63 makes website accessibility mandatory for ALL companies with headquarters or commercial representation in Brazil plus government — broader in scope than the EAA — and the brand-new ABNT NBR 17225:2025 (published March 2025, VERIFIED REAL; ~150 requirements mapped to WCAG 2.2, two conformance levels) finally gives Art. 63 an objective technical yardstick; eMAG (v3.1, 2014) remains the mandatory-but-stale federal-government model. Enforcement is immature but rising: <1–3% of Brazilian sites are accessible (Movimento Web para Todos/BigDataCorp), MPF has formally pushed for Art. 63 regulation, and vendor reports cite growing civil actions in 2025. TECHNICAL SURFACE: screen description is now native to screen readers (JAWS PictureSmart AI uses ChatGPT AND Anthropic Claude; NVDA's open-source AI Content Describer supports GPT/Gemini/Claude/Grok for focus-object, full-screen and camera description), document OCR+chat is mainstream (Seeing AI), AI audio description of video is research-mature (CHI 2025 VideoA11y: AI ≈ trained human annotators, but hallucination + human-oversight caveats), and sign-language translation is honestly immature (SignGemma announced 2025, ASL→English only, early testing; WFD+WASLI formally caution that avatars/MT cannot replace human interpreters). SYNTHESIS FOR VISION MCP: demand is validated both bottom-up (1M+ users, paid tiers, screen-reader vendors embedding the exact same capability) and top-down (EAA + LBI/NBR 17225 create a compliance market for alt-text generation at scale). Natural MCP tools: (1) alt_text_generate (WCAG 1.1.1/NBR 17225-conformant short+long descriptions, decorative-image detection), (2) screen_describe (screenshot/UI description — the JAWS/NVDA pattern), (3) document_read (OCR + structured reading + Q&A — the Seeing AI pattern), (4) scene_qa (conversational follow-up — the Be My AI/Lookout pattern), (5) video_ad_draft (human-in-the-loop audio-description drafts). DUTY OF CARE (sourced): Be My Eyes' own ToS forbids reliance for medication ID/dosing, allergen checks, mobility and emergencies; peer-reviewed research (ASSETS 2024) shows BLV users over-trust confident AI output because they lack ground truth to detect hallucinations — so an assistive vision MCP must ship confidence signaling, hard guardrails on medical/safety/navigation queries, and an escalation path to human verification (the Aira hybrid pattern).","claims":[{"claim":"Be My Eyes announced on March 12, 2026 that it reached 1 million blind and low-vision users and 10 million volunteers, operating in 150+ countries and 180+ languages, and launched the Be My Eyes Foundation.","source":"https://www.bemyeyes.com/news/be-my-eyes-reaches-1-million-blind-and-low-vision-users-and-10-million-volunteers/ (WebFetch)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Largest direct demand proof for AI+human visual description: a seven-figure user base for exactly the service a vision MCP would expose."},{"claim":"In its 10-year anniversary announcement (Jan 2025), Be My Eyes reported 750,000+ BLV users, 8.3M volunteers, 43 million AI and video user requests per year, revenue growth over 300%, 97% customer satisfaction, and enterprise customers including Google, Microsoft, Hilton, Spotify, P&G, Unilever.","source":"https://www.bemyeyes.com/blog/be-my-eyes-celebrates-10-years-and-a-decade-of-accessibility-innovation/ (WebSearch result)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"43M requests/year quantifies call volume for description services; B2B-funded free-to-user model is a viable monetization template."},{"claim":"Be My Eyes' Winter '25 update (Feb 5, 2025; fetched) confirmed 750,000 users / 8.5M volunteers, and added auto read-aloud of AI descriptions, 30-day automatic image deletion, and an opt-out for AI training data.","source":"https://www.bemyeyes.com/news/be-my-eyes-releases-winter-25-app-update-promising-significant-new-capabilities-for-blind-users/ (WebFetch)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Shows privacy/data-retention expectations (auto-deletion, training opt-out) that an assistive vision MCP would be expected to match."},{"claim":"Be My AI (originally 'Virtual Volunteer') was announced March 14, 2023, powered by OpenAI's GPT-4 vision model; 19,000+ BLV beta testers shaped it; it is free for all blind and low-vision users and supports Braille displays (deaf-blind users).","source":"https://www.bemyeyes.com/news/introducing-be-my-ai-formerly-virtual-volunteer-for-people-who-are-blind-or-have-low-vision-powered-by-openais-gpt-4/ and https://www.bemyeyes.com/news/announcing-be-my-ai-soon-available-for-hundreds-of-thousands-of-be-my-eyes-users/ (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"The flagship proof that frontier vision-language models are the engine of modern assistive description — the same capability an MCP server would broker."},{"claim":"In OpenAI's published Be My Eyes case material, only ~10% of users chose to escalate to a human after interacting with the AI tool; Microsoft Disability Answer Desk deployment reportedly resolved 90% of queries with ~4-minute average handling vs 12 for humans.","source":"https://openai.com/index/be-my-eyes/ and https://www.hackster.io/news/be-my-eyes-ties-openai-s-gpt-4-into-its-accessibility-service-and-aims-to-improve-blind-users-cx-1fbd8b86f7a9 (WebSearch results, not independently fetched)","confidence":"medium","why_it_matters":"Quantifies AI-vs-human substitution rate — key unit-economics input for a description service."},{"claim":"Microsoft Seeing AI is a free app on iOS and Android; the Android launch announcement (Microsoft Accessibility Blog) cites 18 languages with a plan for 36 in 2024; recent versions add generative 'rich descriptions' of photos and chat-with-document after scanning.","source":"https://blogs.microsoft.com/accessibility/seeing-ai-app-launches-on-android-including-new-and-updated-features-and-new-languages/ and https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.microsoft.seeingai (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"A big-tech free competitor defines the feature baseline (scene description, OCR+chat, multilingual) any MCP vision tool must meet."},{"claim":"Envision's Ally Solos smart glasses were offered for pre-order at $399 (ultra early bird, sold out) / $499 / $599 tiers on the official site; made by Envision; the Ally assistant reads text, describes scenes, scans documents, and answers questions via open-ear audio.","source":"https://www.ally.me/glasses (WebFetch)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Sub-$600 AI description hardware shows the market moving to always-available wearable description — high-volume, low-latency description APIs are the backend need."},{"claim":"OrCam MyEye 3 Pro (wearable, fully offline, text/face/product recognition plus 'Just Ask' AI) retails in the $3,689–$4,490 range at US resellers, MSRP listed around $4,250.","source":"https://lowvisionsource.com/product/orcam-myeye-3-pro/ and https://nelowvision.com/product/orcam-myeye-3-pro-wearable-ai-reading-aid/ and https://www.orcam.com/en-us/orcam-myeye-3-pro (WebSearch results)","confidence":"medium","why_it_matters":"Proves users/insurers pay thousands of dollars for offline visual description — strong willingness-to-pay evidence at the premium end."},{"claim":"Aira's personal visual-interpreting subscriptions (official pricing page, fetched) run $26/mo (20 min Silver) to $1,160/mo (880 min Platinum); Access AI image descriptions are 'free for all users' with the option to verify AI responses with a human visual interpreter; Aira kept 2025 prices flat and added ≥10% minutes to Metal plans; Aira also announced compatibility with Meta AI glasses.","source":"https://aira.io/subscriptions/ (WebFetch); https://aira.io/exciting-pricing-news-for-aira-subscribers/ and https://aira.io/aira-now-works-with-ai-glasses-from-meta/ (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Hard pricing data: humans-on-demand cost ~$1.3/min while AI description is free — the AI+human-escalation hybrid is the proven product architecture for an assistive MCP."},{"claim":"Google Lookout remains an active, free Android app in 2025; its Images mode captures/describes images with follow-up Q&A powered by Gemini (descriptions global in English; Q&A in US/UK/Canada English), though community reviewers note it appears to use an older Gemini model than TalkBack; Google's GAAD 2025 announcement brought Gemini-powered image descriptions to TalkBack.","source":"https://support.google.com/accessibility/android/answer/9031274 and https://accessibleandroid.com/google-lookout-current-offerings-and-the-untapped-potential-for-a-blind-essential-toolbox/ and https://blog.google/company-news/outreach-and-initiatives/accessibility/android-gemini-ai-gaad-2025/ (WebSearch results)","confidence":"medium","why_it_matters":"All three OS vendors (Microsoft, Google, Apple-ecosystem apps) now bundle AI description — validating the capability while pushing differentiation toward compliance/API/vertical use."},{"claim":"WHO (fact sheet dated 10 Feb 2026): at least 2.2 billion people globally have a near or distance vision impairment; at least 1 billion cases were preventable or unaddressed; annual global productivity loss ≈ US$411 billion.","source":"https://www.who.int/news-room/fact-sheets/detail/blindness-and-visual-impairment (WebFetch)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Top-down market sizing for assistive description demand; Be My Eyes itself cites 340M blind/low-vision people (https://www.bemyeyes.com/about-us/, WebFetch)."},{"claim":"WCAG 2.2 Success Criterion 1.1.1 (Non-text Content, Level A) requires that all non-text content presented to the user has a text alternative serving the equivalent purpose, with listed exceptions (controls need a descriptive name; decorative images get empty alt).","source":"https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-content.html (WebSearch result, W3C official)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"The normative anchor for an alt_text_generate MCP tool — output must 'serve the equivalent purpose', not just caption."},{"claim":"Meta/Facebook has generated automatic alt text (AAT) via object recognition since 2016; the 2021 update recognizes 1,200+ concepts (10x the original) and produces descriptions like 'May be a selfie of 2 people, outdoors, the Leaning Tower of Pisa', exposed to any screen reader via standard alt text.","source":"https://about.fb.com/news/2021/01/using-ai-to-improve-photo-descriptions-for-blind-and-visually-impaired-people/ and https://www.facebook.com/help/216219865403298 (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Proves platforms already auto-generate alt text at billions-of-images scale — and that generic tag-level AAT leaves a quality gap a VLM-based MCP can fill."},{"claim":"The European Accessibility Act (Directive 2019/882) became enforceable on 28 June 2025 across all 27 EU member states, covering e-commerce, banking, transport, telecom, e-books and consumer devices; it applies to non-EU companies selling to EU consumers; existing services have a transition until 28 June 2030; reported penalties vary by member state (figures cited include up to €100,000 and 'up to three million euros' plus market withdrawal).","source":"https://accessible-eu-centre.ec.europa.eu/content-corner/news/eaa-comes-effect-june-2025-are-you-ready-2025-01-31_en and https://www.gtlaw.com/en/insights/2025/7/european-accessibility-act-compliance-what-businesses-in-the-eu-market-need-to-know and https://www.levelaccess.com/compliance-overview/european-accessibility-act-eaa/ (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Creates a legally compelled, continent-wide buyer base for alt-text/description automation; penalty figures differ per member state, so treat exact amounts as jurisdiction-specific."},{"claim":"EAA microenterprise exemption applies to SERVICES only: businesses with fewer than 10 employees AND annual turnover/balance sheet ≤ €2 million are exempt from service requirements but not product requirements; the exemption vanishes immediately when thresholds are crossed.","source":"https://www.webyes.com/blogs/eaa-exemptions/ and https://www.taylorwessing.com/en/interface/2025/accessibility/key-eu-accessibility-act-exemptions-and-the-challenges-they-pose (WebSearch results)","confidence":"medium","why_it_matters":"Defines the compliance-market boundary: SMBs above 10 employees selling into the EU are in scope — a long-tail customer segment for automated alt-text tooling."},{"claim":"EAA enforcement began immediately: within days of the June 2025 deadline, French disability advocacy organizations issued formal legal notices to four major grocery retailers demanding accessible e-commerce platforms by September 2025.","source":"https://www.pivotalaccessibility.com/2025/09/eaa-enforcement-in-europe-following-the-june-2025-deadline/ (WebSearch result)","confidence":"medium","why_it_matters":"Evidence enforcement is real, advocacy-driven, and fast — compliance urgency translates into demand for remediation tooling."},{"claim":"Brazil's LBI (Lei 13.146/2015, Estatuto da Pessoa com Deficiência), Art. 63: accessibility is MANDATORY on websites maintained by companies with headquarters or commercial representation in Brazil and by government bodies, 'according to the best practices and accessibility guidelines adopted internationally'; §1 requires a visible accessibility symbol; §§2–3 cover telecenters/lan houses (≥10% accessible machines).","source":"https://www.jusbrasil.com.br/topicos/49549565/artigo-63-da-lei-n-13146-de-06-de-julho-de-2015 and https://www2.camara.leg.br/legin/fed/lei/2015/lei-13146-6-julho-2015-781174-publicacaooriginal-147468-pl.html (WebSearch results; planalto.gov.br fetch failed with socket error, text corroborated by Câmara dos Deputados official source)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Brazil's obligation is broader than the EAA (ALL companies with Brazilian presence, no sector list) — a huge addressable compliance market in the project's home jurisdiction."},{"claim":"ABNT NBR 17225 EXISTS and is real: published March 2025 (launch reported 11 March 2025), titled 'Acessibilidade Digital — Requisitos de conformidade para páginas e aplicações web'; ~150 requirements/recommendations based on WCAG 2.2; defines two conformance levels (Conformidade Regular and Conformidade Plena); developed over ~2 years by 178 specialists with Ceweb.br coordination and federal government support; positioned as the technical yardstick for LBI Art. 63.","source":"https://www.gov.br/mdh/pt-br/assuntos/noticias/2025/marco/com-apoio-do-governo-federal-nova-norma-tecnica-da-abnt-e-instituida-para-impulsionar-acessibilidade-digital-no-brasil and https://cta.ifrs.edu.br/abnt-nbr-17225-2025-acessibilidade-em-conteudo-e-aplicacoes-web-requisitos/ and https://www.cgi.br/noticia/releases/desenvolvida-com-coordenacao-do-ceweb-br-nova-norma-da-abnt-estabelece-requisitos-para-melhorar-a-acessibilidade-de-sites/ (WebSearch results; its latest version is 3.1 from April 2014 — i.e., it is a decade stale, with NBR 17225 now the modern reference.","source":"https://emag.governoeletronico.gov.br/ and https://www.gov.br/governodigital/pt-br/acessibilidade-e-usuario/acessibilidade-digital/modelo-de-acessibilidade and https://cta.ifrs.edu.br/modelo-de-acessibilidade-em-governo-eletronico-emag-3-1/ (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Shows the Brazilian public-sector baseline and the modernization gap NBR 17225 fills — relevant for selling to government integrators."},{"claim":"Brazilian enforcement reality: fewer than 3% of Brazilian websites meet accessibility standards per Movimento Web para Todos, with a BigDataCorp/MWPT study citing only 0.74% fully accessible; default GPT usage is free via PollinationsAI; it also does camera face-framing.","source":"https://github.com/cartertemm/AI-content-describer and https://nvda-addons.org/addon.php?id=344 (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Open-source prior art for screen description tooling: shows exact UX primitives (focus/navigator/screen/camera) an MCP screen_describe tool should expose, and that the community is provider-agnostic."},{"claim":"AI audio description of video: the VideoA11y study (CHI 2025-era research, evaluated with 347 sighted participants and 40 BLV users against 7 professional describers) found AI descriptions outperform novice human annotations and are comparable to trained humans on clarity/accuracy/objectivity; but the literature flags object hallucinations and keeps human oversight central (Describe Now, DIS 2025; DescribePro 2025; ADCanvas 2025-26 enabling BLV creators).","source":"https://visonicai.com/stories/ai-for-audio-description-complete-guide-2026/ and https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3715336.3735685 and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.01092 and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2411.11835 (WebSearch results)","confidence":"medium","why_it_matters":"Audio description is research-mature but production-immature: an MCP video_ad_draft tool should be positioned as human-in-the-loop drafting, not autonomous AD."},{"claim":"Sign-language tech maturity (honest): Google DeepMind announced SignGemma (May 2025), an on-device-capable ASL→English text translation model, still in early testing with public availability 'expected by end of 2025' via interest form; current 2026 release status not verified)","confidence":"medium","why_it_matters":"Sign-language translation is pre-product: do NOT scope it into a v1 vision MCP; one-directional, one-language, unreleased."},{"claim":"The World Federation of the Deaf and WASLI issued a joint formal statement cautioning against signing avatars / machine translation as replacements for human interpreters: signed languages are full languages requiring cultural/contextual translation that current computer-generated output cannot render.","source":"https://wfdeaf.org/wfd-wasli-issue-statement-signing-avatars/ and https://wfdeaf.org/resources/statement-on-use-of-signing-avatars/ (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Community-authoritative red line: an assistive MCP marketing 'sign language support' would face legitimate Deaf-community pushback — exclude or clearly scope as experimental."},{"claim":"Duty-of-care precedent — Be My Eyes' own Terms of Service and help docs instruct users NOT to use Be My AI for critical health/safety: no medication identification or dosing, no allergen checks; the service 'is not designed or intended to be used as a mobility aid' (no replacement for cane/guide dog) and not for medical/emergency use; users 'agree not to rely on the services', provided 'as is'.","source":"https://www.bemyeyes.com/terms-of-service/ and https://support.bemyeyes.com/hc/en-us/articles/18133134809105-How-do-I-use-Be-My-AI (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"The market leader's exact legal guardrails are the template for an assistive vision MCP: category-level refusals (medication, allergens, navigation, emergencies) plus as-is disclaimers."},{"claim":"Peer-reviewed research documents overtrust harm: blind users were found to over-trust AI image captions 'even when the output made little sense'; BLV users often lack ground truth to distinguish a bad photo from a bad prediction; participants in ASSETS 2024 studies said Be My AI's humanistic, confident tone may encourage overtrusting hallucinated output, and called for explicit uncertainty conveyance ('Misfitting With AI: How Blind People Verify and Contest AI Errors'; 'I look at it as the king of knowledge', 19 blind GenAI users interviewed).","source":"https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3663548.3675659 and https://dl.acm.org/doi/fullHtml/10.1145/3663548.3675631 and https://arxiv.org/pdf/2403.15604 and https://sciencenews.dk/en/when-ai-sounds-certain-blind-users-have-to-trust-it-even-when-it-is-guessing (WebSearch results)","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Sourced proof that wrong descriptions cause real-world harm risk for dependent users: mandates confidence scoring, hedged language for low-certainty content, and human-verification escalation in any assistive MCP tool."},{"claim":"SYNTHESIS: assistive tech VALIDATES demand for a vision+description MCP service on three independent axes: (1) usage — 1M+ BLV users on Be My Eyes alone, 43M+ requests/yr, free big-tech apps (Seeing AI, Lookout) plus $399–$4,250 hardware and $26–$1,160/mo human services; (2) capability convergence — screen readers (JAWS, NVDA) already call OpenAI/Anthropic vision APIs for screen description, i.e., the exact MCP tool shape; (3) regulation — WCAG 2.2 SC 1.1.1 + EAA (enforced since 06/2025) + Brazil LBI Art. 63 + ABNT NBR 17225:2025 legally compel alt text at scale with ~99% of Brazilian sites non-compliant. The MCP tool set that maps 1:1 to proven products: alt_text_generate (compliance vertical), screen_describe (JAWS/NVDA pattern), document_read OCR+Q&A (Seeing AI pattern), scene_qa with conversational follow-up (Be My AI/Lookout pattern), video_ad_draft human-in-loop (research-stage). Mandatory duty-of-care layer: category refusals (medication/allergen/mobility/emergency — Be My Eyes ToS precedent), uncertainty signaling (ASSETS 2024 findings), and human-escalation hooks (Aira's free-AI + paid-human-verify hybrid).","source":"Synthesis of all fetched/searched sources cited in the claims above","confidence":"high","why_it_matters":"Directly answers the dual research goal: the primary paper was not fetched in this pass."]},"verdicts":[{"claim":"Be My Eyes announced on March 12, 2026 that it reached 1 million blind and low-vision users and 10 million volunteers, operating in 150+ countries and 180+ languages, and launched the Be My Eyes Foundation.","verdict":"confirmed","evidence":"Direct fetch of the announcement confirms the dateline 'San Francisco, March 12, 2026' at the 41st CSUN Assistive Technology Conference, with exact statements of 'more than 1 million blind or low-vision users... over 10 million volunteers', 'over 150 countries... more than 180 languages', and 'announcing the creation of the Be My Eyes Foundation' (bemyeyes.com/news/be-my-eyes-reaches-1-million-blind-and-low-vision-users-and-10-million-volunteers/). > 11	
> 12	---
> 13	
> 14	## 1. Sumário Executivo
> 15	
> 16	### O que é
> 17	
> 18	O **Vision MCP** é um servidor **Model Context Protocol nativo do MCORCH** que expõe uma camada de **percepção visual + deepsearch fundamentado** tanto para os agentes internos (Pantheon: engineer/scientist/artisan/marketing-growth) quanto para **ecossistemas externos** (Claude Desktop/Code, VS Code, ChatGPT/connector, Claude API), via um endpoint remoto **Streamable HTTP + OAuth 2.1**. Ele transforma imagem, vídeo, captura de tela e páginas web em (a) descrições/detecções/segmentações/OCR estruturados e (b) **referências de mercado consolidadas** extraídas por deepsearch e gravadas na Knowledge Mesh.
> 19	
> 20	A arquitetura de produto é desenhada sobre uma metáfora biológica defensável — a **"Essência Humana"**: uma periferia barata, always-on, inspirada em princípios neuromórficos emulados em software (detecção de mudança/saliência) que **dispara** uma análise foveal cara e profunda apenas quando há sinal — exatamente como a retina humana concentra ~5% de cones de alta acuidade em uma fóvea minúscula e usa ~95% de bastões para vigilância periférica de baixa resolução ([NCBI Webvision](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK554706/); [StatPearls Retina](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK545310/)).
> 21	
> 22	### Por que agora
> 23	
> 24	1. **A especificação MCP amadureceu.** A revisão corrente é **2025-11-25** (marcada "Current"), define exatamente dois transportes — stdio e **Streamable HTTP** (o antigo HTTP+SSE está deprecado) — e formaliza OAuth 2.1 com RFC 9728 Protected Resource Metadata para servidores remotos protegidos ([MCP Transports 2025-11-25](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports); [MCP Authorization 2025-11-25](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/authorization)). O Claude API MCP connector (`mcp-client-2025-11-20`) consome servidores remotos HTTPS com bearer token ([Claude MCP connector](https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/agents-and-tools/mcp-connector)).
> 25	2. **O MCORCH já roda um servidor MCP in-house.** `mcorch_gitnexus` usa `StreamableHTTPServerTransport` em `127.0.0.1:3100` dentro do Sovereign Mesh hardened — confirmado materialmente em `packages/gitnexus-core/src/mcp/server.ts:16,97` e `docker-compose.yml:89-119`. O runtime recomendado é uma extensão de infraestrutura provada, não terreno novo.
> 26	3. **Toda a cadeia de billing/tenancy/sentinel/mesh já existe** e é reutilizável (Seção 5).
> 27	
> 28	### O diferencial — sendo honesto sobre o que a recon do sandcastles.ai estabeleceu
> 29	
> 30	A recon do **sandcastles.ai** foi **materialmente verificada (alta confiança)** por probes diretos (curl, 2026-06-10/11), e **confirmada por re-probe adversarial independente**:
> 31	
> 32	- O MCP deles é **remoto, Streamable HTTP, OAuth-protegido** em `https://mcp.sandcastles.ai/`: `POST initialize` retorna `HTTP 401` com `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer ... resource_metadata="https://mcp.sandcastles.ai/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource"` (RFC 9728), servido por "Google Frontend" ([probe + verdict confirmado](https://mcp.sandcastles.ai/)). O authorization server (`signin.sandcastles.ai`) anuncia DCR, PKCE S256, device-code e refresh tokens — o que faz "cole 1 URL → login no browser → conectado" funcionar com zero config ([metadata chain confirmada](https://signin.sandcastles.ai/.well-known/openid-configuration)).
> 33	- O **diferencial de distribuição** (confirmado verbatim por render headless do doc oficial): eles não enviam só o servidor MCP — enviam um **"Plugin Zip" versionado** baixado de Settings → Connectors, que empacota o connector remoto (URL pré-preenchida) **+ um conjunto curado de Skills nomeadas** (slash-commands tipo `/analyze`, `/rules`) que envelopam as ferramentas cruas em workflows guiados ([setup doc confirmado](https://help.sandcastles.ai/mcp)). É um **triângulo de paridade de três superfícies** (UI web = REST API = MCP tools) + **skills-as-distribution**.
> 34	
> 35	**O que a recon NÃO estabeleceu (e portanto não é diferencial nosso):**
> 36	
> 37	- O provedor de auth é **inferido** como WorkOS AuthKit (path `/user_management/`, `client_id` ULID), **não declarado** — confiança média, citar como "consistente com", nunca como fato.
> 38	- Receita ~$1.6M / 22.000 creators vem de **uma única fonte secundária (Medium)** — confiança baixa, usar como anedota no MRD, jamais como evidência de mercado ([Medium, não-verificado](https://anirudhchundawat.medium.com/how-1-6m-app-started-with-a-one-day-experiment-without-coding-a70ec0d31441)).
> 39	- **Crucial:** sandcastles valida o **padrão de distribuição** (remote MCP + plugin + skills), **não o mercado de vision-deepsearch**. A varredura competitiva ao vivo do registry oficial (2026-06-11) provou que as duas metades do nosso "diferencial" já são servidas por incumbentes first-party:
> 40	  - **Vision sobre MCP já está tomado:** `com.roboflow/roboflow-mcp` (v1.0.3, "Roboflow Official", hosted em `mcp.roboflow.com/mcp`) expõe ~70 ferramentas de CV e **já executa o mesmíssimo padrão "remote MCP + Apps + Skills"** ([registry probe](https://registry.modelcontextprotocol.io/v0/servers?search=roboflow&limit=20); [llms.txt](https://mcp.roboflow.com/llms.txt)).
> 41	  - **Deepsearch sobre MCP já está tomado:** `ai.exa/exa`, `io.github.firecrawl/firecrawl-mcp-server` (6.5k★, MIT, self-hostable) e `io.github.tavily-ai/tavily-mcp` têm demanda comprovada — npm downloads no último mês: firecrawl-mcp **376.976**, tavily-mcp **165.112**, exa-mcp-server **67.928** ([npm stats](https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/point/last-month/firecrawl-mcp)).
> 42	
> 43	**Logo, o diferencial defensável do Vision MCP se sustenta por mérito próprio em quatro eixos, não na cópia do sandcastles:**
> 44	
> 45	1. **O produto cruzado** — saída de visão **fundamentada/verificada por evidência de deepsearch** numa única superfície de ferramenta. Busca direta não encontrou nenhum servidor MCP combinando computer vision com agentes de deep-research (whitespace genuíno) — **mas ausência de competidor é também ausência de validação de demanda**: o MRD deve tratar isso como **hipótese a validar**, não mercado provado.
> 46	2. **Soberania / self-host** vs o servidor hosted-only da Roboflow (cujo repo GitHub retorna 404 — provável closed-source) — deploy on-prem no Sovereign Mesh é uma cunha real.
> 47	3. **Credencial per-tenant + billing mcoCoins atômico** que o MCORCH já tem nativo (Seção 5).
> 48	4. **A filosofia "Essência Humana"** — arquitetura de percepção em dois níveis (Seção 3) como princípio de custo/eficiência, fundamentada em biologia e já validada como paradigma de engenharia em XR.
> 49	
> 50	---
> 51	
> 52	## 2. Os 4 Pilares
> 53	
> 54	> Para cada pilar: fundamentos consolidados + **veredictos da verificação adversarial**. Refutados aparecem corrigidos; não-verificáveis são marcados `⚠️`.
> 55	
> 56	### Pilar I — Computer Vision (CV)
> 57	
> 58	**Fundamento.** Para um SaaS server-side sem GPU garantida, a CV de produção se divide em quatro pistas: VLM-via-API (default sem GPU), foundation models de segmentação/open-vocab (GPU/hosted), stacks clássicos/treináveis (CPU), e OCR/embeddings.
> 59	
> 60	| Afirmação | Veredicto adversarial | Fonte |
> 61	|---|---|---|
> 62	| **SAM 3** é a geração corrente do Segment Anything (lançada **2025-11-19**); **SAM 3.1** "Object Multiplex" em **2026-03-27**. Adiciona prompts de conceito por **texto/exemplar** (detect+segment+track de todas as instâncias). | ✅ **Confirmado** (Meta newsroom + Roboflow + Ultralytics + Voxel51). Citar "SAM 2 como último" está uma geração atrás. | [github.com/facebookresearch/sam3](https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3) · [ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3](https://ai.meta.com/blog/segment-anything-model-3/) |
> 63	| SAM 3 exige **CUDA ≥12.6 / PyTorch ≥2.7 / Python ≥3.12** — **não é opção CPU-only**. | ✅ Confirmado (guias de deploy Spheron/StableLearn). Para SaaS sem GPU: consumir via host (fal.ai $0.005/req, Replicate). | [Verdict](https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam3) |
> 64	| SAM 3 sob **"SAM License" bespoke** (não Apache); SAM 2 é **Apache-2.0**. | ✅ Confirmado — **regressão de licença entre gerações**. SAM 2 é o fallback de licença limpa. | [SAM 2 LICENSE](https://github.com/facebookresearch/sam2/blob/main/LICENSE) · [SAM License raw](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/facebookresearch/sam3/main/LICENSE) |
> 65	| **DINOv3** (ago/2025): backbone self-supervised SOTA, porém **licença comercial gated** (não Apache como DINOv2). | ✅ Confirmado; impõe **atribuição "Built with DINOv3"** + direito de terminação Meta. DINOv2 permanece a opção permissiva. | [DINOv3 License](https://ai.meta.com/resources/models-and-libraries/dinov3-license/) |
> 66	| **Ultralytics (YOLO)** é **AGPL-3.0 OU Enterprise paga** — a maior armadilha de licença do ecossistema para SaaS closed-source. | ✅ Confirmado **diretamente** (dual-licensing). ⚠️ **"YOLO26 lançado jan/2026" NÃO foi verificado** (veio de síntese de busca, não fetch direto). Tratar o nome/data YOLO26 como não-confirmado; a armadilha AGPL é fato. | [github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics](https://github.com/ultralytics/ultralytics) |
> 67	| **RF-DETR** (Roboflow, ICLR 2026): detecção+segmentação real-time, **Apache-2.0** nos tamanhos core (Nano..Large); só XL/2XL "Plus" sob PML 1.0. | ✅ Confirmado — alternativa de licença limpa ao YOLO/AGPL. | [github.com/roboflow/rf-detr](https://github.com/roboflow/rf-detr) |
> 68	| **Gemini API** detecta objetos nativamente, retornando boxes `[ymin,xmin,ymax,xmax]` normalizados 0–1000. | ✅ Alta confiança (doc oficial). Único VLM-API com box detection treinado-in. | [ai.google.dev/.../image-understanding](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/image-understanding) |
> 69	| Segmentação (máscaras) **não suportada** em Gemini 3 Pro/Flash; rotear para Gemini 2.5 Flash. | ⚠️ **Confiança média** — vem de snippet; a página fetchada apenas **omite** segmentação, não declara a limitação. Validar antes de fixar no SDD. | [ai.google.dev/.../gemini-3](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/gemini-3) |
> 70	| **OpenCV 5.0** (jun/2026): primeiro major desde 2018; C API legado removido, DNN reescrita (ONNX >80%). | ⚠️ **Contradição interna sinalizada pela crítica:** rotulado "consolidado" sendo de dias atrás; **licença do 5.0 não re-verificada**. O SDD precisa de **decisão explícita pin-4.x-vs-5.0**. | [opencv.org/opencv-5](https://opencv.org/opencv-5/) |
> 71	| **PaddleOCR 3.x** Apache-2.0, **CPU-viável**, 100+ idiomas (PP-OCRv5) + doc→Markdown/JSON. | ✅ Confirmado — escolha OCR consolidada/permissiva quando não há GPU. | [github.com/PaddlePaddle/PaddleOCR](https://github.com/PaddlePaddle/PaddleOCR) |
> 72	| **Mistral OCR 3** — pricing era conflitante. | ✅ **Resolvido (fetch ao vivo):** **$2/1.000 páginas** (extração) + **$3/1.000** (annotations) no tier standard; **$1/1.000** é o Batch API; os $0.50 eram a geração anterior. **Não está no OpenRouter** → exige integração direta Mistral (chave per-user). | [mistral.ai/pricing](https://mistral.ai/pricing) · [OCR 3 launch](https://mistral.ai/news/mistral-ocr-3/) |
> 73	| Pista permissiva leve: **Florence-2** (MIT, 230M/770M, caption/detect/ground/OCR), **Moondream** (Apache-2.0 0.5B/2B, roda em CPU/RPi), **SigLIP 2** e **CLIP** (MIT) para embeddings. | ✅ Consolidado. Florence-2 é o modelo mais denso em MIT pequeno o suficiente para CPU. | [Florence-2](https://huggingface.co/microsoft/Florence-2-large) · [Moondream](https://moondream.ai/blog/introducing-moondream-0-5b) · [SigLIP 2](https://huggingface.co/blog/siglip2) |
> 74	| **Qwen3-VL** (8B→235B) via OpenRouter; instruct weights **Apache-2.0** confirmados no card HF. | ✅ Confirmado — VLM frontier na pista Apache sem propagação de licença. | [Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct) |
> 75	
> 76	**Veredicto de hype-vs-consolidado:** consolidados = VLM via OpenRouter, OpenCV (com pin), PaddleOCR, transformers/timm, SigLIP2/CLIP, Grounding DINO, SAM 2. Jovens-mas-reais = SAM 3/3.1, RF-DETR, DeepSeek-OCR-2, Perception Encoder. Emergentes = wrappers "agentic CV", SAM 3D — adotar atrás de interfaces.
> 77	
> 78	### Pilar II — Análise de Movimento (Motion)
> 79	
> 80	| Afirmação | Veredicto adversarial | Fonte |
> 81	|---|---|---|
> 82	| Fluxo óptico é linhagem **RAFT**: RAFT (BSD-3, ECCV 2020) → **SEA-RAFT** (BSD-3, ECCV 2024, ≥2.3× mais rápido, SOTA Spring 3.69 EPE) → **MEMFOF** (ICCV 2025, multi-frame em 2.09 GB a 1080p). | ✅ Confirmado. **Gap fechado:** a licença do MEMFOF — antes "não-verificada" — é **BSD-3-Clause** (verdict). | [RAFT](https://github.com/princeton-vl/RAFT) · [SEA-RAFT](https://github.com/princeton-vl/SEA-RAFT) · [MEMFOF](https://github.com/msu-video-group/memfof) |
> 83	| **RTMPose-m**: 75.8 AP COCO a **90+ FPS em CPU Intel i7-11700** (Apache-2.0, ONNX/ncnn/OpenVINO); **MediaPipe Pose** (33 landmarks, Apache-2.0). | ✅ Confirmado — pose multi-pessoa vira workload **CPU-barato**. ⚠️ **Crítica:** todos os números CPU são **x86**; o host-alvo é **Oracle ARM64** — **faltam benchmarks aarch64** (ONNX/ncnn ARM). | [MMPose/RTMPose](https://github.com/open-mmlab/mmpose/tree/main/projects/rtmpose) · [MediaPipe](https://github.com/google-ai-edge/mediapipe) |
> 84	| Tracking consolidado tracking-by-detection: **ByteTrack** (MIT) e **BoT-SORT** (MIT, default na Ultralytics YOLO). | ✅ Confirmado — camada de associação MOT barata sobre qualquer detector. | [ByteTrack](https://github.com/ifzhang/ByteTrack) · [BoT-SORT](https://github.com/NirAharon/BoT-SORT) · [Ultralytics Track](https://docs.ultralytics.com/modes/track/) |
> 85	| **VideoMAE V2** (action recognition self-host, repo **MIT**); **InternVideo2** (92.1% K400) — licença antes não-verificada. | ✅ **Gap fechado:** repo InternVideo **Apache-2.0**, weights **InternVideo2-Stage2_6B tagged MIT** — pista permissiva, mas **fixar o checkpoint exato** (licenças variam por variante). | [VideoMAE V2 LICENSE](https://github.com/OpenGVLab/VideoMAEv2/blob/master/LICENSE) · [InternVideo LICENSE](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/OpenGVLab/InternVideo/main/LICENSE) · [Stage2_6B](https://huggingface.co/OpenGVLab/InternVideo2-Stage2_6B) |
> 86	| **Gemini é o único LLM-API geral com input nativo de vídeo**: 1 FPS default, **~300 tokens/seg** (default res), até 1h (3h low-res). **OpenAI NÃO tem input nativo de vídeo** (mar/2026, workaround ffmpeg→frames perde sinal temporal). | ✅ Confirmado (doc Gemini + issue OpenAI). Alternativa video-nativa: **TwelveLabs** (Marengo/Pegasus) via Amazon Bedrock. | [Gemini video](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/video-understanding) · [OpenAI issue #1778](https://github.com/openai/openai-node/issues/1778) |
> 87	| **OpenCV MOG2/KNN** (background subtraction): máscara de foreground por frame para câmera estática — gate quase-grátis em CPU **antes** de estágios caros. | ✅ Confirmado — o nicho durável dos clássicos: câmera fixa, CPU-only, objetos sub-detector, pré-filtro para cortar gasto GPU/API. | [OpenCV BGS tutorial](https://docs.opencv.org/4.x/d1/dc5/tutorial_background_subtraction.html) |
> 88	
> 89	**Stack consolidado de pipeline:** MOG2/frame-diff gate (CPU) → RTMPose/MediaPipe pose (CPU) → YOLO+BoT-SORT/ByteTrack tracking (GPU) → VideoMAE V2 fine-tune para ações fechadas (GPU) → Gemini video API para perguntas temporais abertas (API), com SAM 2/3 reservado para tracking promptável pixel-accurate.
> 90	
> 91	### Pilar III — Sensores Neuromórficos (Neuromorphic)
> 92	
> 93	| Afirmação | Veredicto adversarial | Fonte |
> 94	|---|---|---|
> 95	| Sensores event-based são **produto comercial real**: Sony vende 4 EVS (IMX636/646/637/647, 4.86µm, 1.06 Geps, latência µs), co-dev Prophesee. | ✅ Confirmado (Sony + FRAMOS + IDS + Lucid). | [Sony EVS](https://www.sony-semicon.com/en/products/is/industry/evs.html) |
> 96	| **O mercado é financeiramente frágil:** Prophesee entrou em **redressement judiciaire** (recuperação judicial FR) fim de 2024; reestruturou em 2025 (novo CEO, pivô para defesa/industrial). | ✅ Confirmado (Sifted + VIPress). ⚠️ **Desfecho legal formal (plano de continuação vs venda) não-verificado.** | [Insolvency](http://image-sensors-world.blogspot.com/2025/01/prophesee-files-for-insolvency.html) · [Recap 2025](https://www.prophesee.ai/2026/01/07/prophesee-recap-2025/) |
> 97	| **Intel arquivou TODO o framework Lava em 2026-05-13** — sem manutenção. Loihi 2/Hala Point é research-only (INRC). | ✅ Confirmado — **diretiva de engenharia: NÃO adotar Lava em 2026.** | [github.com/lava-nc/lava](https://github.com/lava-nc/lava) |
> 98	| **BrainChip Akida Cloud** (ago/2025): inferência neuromórfica como serviço cloud (free tier + usage-based) — **o único processador neuromórfico consumível como API sem hardware**. | ✅ Confirmado. AKD1500 só atinge volume Q3'26. | [Akida Cloud](https://brainchip.com/brainchip-launches-akida-cloud-for-instant-access-to-latest-akida-neuromorphic-technology/) |
> 99	| **Os PRINCÍPIOS neuromórficos são software-emuláveis** sobre vídeo/screenshots: **v2e** (MIT) converte vídeo em event streams DVS realistas; **rpg_vid2e/ESIM** (GPL-3.0) idem; **OpenEB/Metavision** (Apache-2.0) traz simulador Video-to-Event de primeira classe; **CARLA** tem sensor DVS embutido. Stack SNN completo é PyTorch puro: **snnTorch** (MIT), Norse, SpikingJelly, Tonic. | ✅ Confirmado — a própria Prophesee trata síntese frame→evento como ferramenta de produção. | [v2e](https://github.com/SensorsINI/v2e) · [OpenEB](https://github.com/prophesee-ai/openeb) · [snnTorch](https://github.com/jeshraghian/snntorch) |
> 100	| **Onde o hardware é insubstituível (honestidade):** (a) resolução temporal µs verdadeira — eventos sintéticos herdam o frame-rate da fonte; interpolação **fabrica** timestamps inter-frame, não os **observa** (a própria framing do v2e: "higher EFFECTIVE timing precision"); (b) HDR >140dB e ausência de motion blur na captura; (c) economia de potência/banda no sensor (GenX320 a 36µW). | ✅ Confirmado — qualquer claim de produto deve dizer **"event-like processing"**, jamais "microsecond sensing". | [v2e paper](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2006.07722) · [GenX320](https://www.prophesee.ai/2023/10/16/prophesee-launches-genx320/) |
> 101	| O gap sim-to-real (modelos treinados em eventos sintéticos degradam em sensores reais) **se dissolve quando o domínio de deploy É vídeo/tela renderizada** (train-domain == inference-domain). | ⚠️ **Raciocínio plausível, NÃO evidência** — nenhuma fonte testou pipelines de evento sintético em conteúdo de tela especificamente. **Marcar como HIPÓTESE no SDD**, não como achado. | [Sim-to-real study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13722) |
> 102	
> 103	**Receita pragmática 2026 para SaaS sem sensor:** síntese de eventos estilo OpenEB/v2e (ou frame-differencing log-intensity barato) → representações voxel-grid/time-surface → modelos GPU convencionais ou SNNs snnTorch/SpikingJelly, com Akida Cloud opcional se inferência neuromórfica genuína for desejada.
> 104	
> 105	### Pilar IV — Visão Periférica / Foveada (Peripheral/Foveated)
> 106	
> 107	| Afirmação | Veredicto adversarial | Fonte |
> 108	|---|---|---|
> 109	| A retina é radicalmente não-uniforme: cones foveais ~170–200k/mm² vs periferia <20k/mm²; **~95% dos fotorreceptores são bastões** (vigilância ampla), ~5% cones (acuidade central esparsa). | ✅ Confirmado (Curcio/AOSLO; Nature; Webvision). Justifica gastar compute hi-res num crop minúsculo e lo-res no campo amplo. | [Webvision Fovea](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK554706/) · [Retina](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/NBK545310/) |
> 110	| A periferia é **detector de movimento/mudança**: o stream dorsal "where/how" recebe **~90%** do input do sistema magnocelular (alto ganho de contraste, baixa freq. espacial, rápido), projeta a V5/MT e córtex parietal, dirige atenção e movimentos oculares. | ✅ Confirmado **com nuance**: "~90%" é aproximação de livro-texto ("dominado por/grande maioria"), não valor exato pinado. Substância correta. | [Magnocellular/dorsal](https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC4186266/) |
> 111	| **Two-streams hypothesis** (Goodale & Milner 1992): ventral "what" (identidade) vs dorsal "where/how" (localização/ação) — paciente D.F. Mapeia direto no design de dois níveis. **Feature Integration Theory** (Treisman 1980): features básicas pré-atentivas em paralelo ("pop-out" em tempo constante); conjunções exigem busca serial atencional. | ✅ Ambas confirmadas (Frontiers; Wikipedia; CUNY). Blueprint biológico direto do nível barato (pop-out paralelo) vs caro (binding serial). | [Two-streams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-streams_hypothesis) · [FIT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feature_integration_theory) |
> 112	| **Foveated rendering eye-tracked é paradigma de engenharia EM PRODUÇÃO** em XR: Apple Vision Pro, Quest Pro, PSVR2, Vive Pro Eye. No Quest Pro, ETFR economiza **33–45% GPU** (default), limitado por **~50ms** de latência de eye-tracking. | ✅ Confirmado (Meta dev docs + UploadVR). A latência do trigger limita o ganho do tier caro-sob-demanda — **lição de sistemas para o loop saliência→fóvea**. ⚠️ A figura visionOS "72%" é **não-verificada** (blog secundário). | [Quest Pro ETFR](https://www.uploadvr.com/quest-pro-foveated-rendering-performance/) · [Meta ETFR](https://developers.meta.com/horizon/blog/save-gpu-with-eye-tracked-foveated-rendering/) |
> 113	| Componentes ML maduros: **saliência** consolidada (DeepGaze IIE ICCV 2021, DeepGaze III 2022, SALICON); **glimpse/hard-attention** (RAM, Mnih 2014); arquiteturas peripheral-inspired (**PerViT** NeurIPS 2022; modelo foveal-peripheral Frontiers 2021 ~10× mais rápido, 0.39% perda); o loop "passe barato amplo → análise focada cara" é dominante em VLMs hi-res (**V*/SEAL**, Chain-of-Focus, CropVLM). | ✅ Confirmados individualmente. ⚠️ **Crítica — o maior buraco de risco técnico do diferencial:** DeepGaze é treinado em **free-viewing de imagens naturais** — **sem evidência de transferência para screenshots/UIs/conteúdo renderizado** (datasets de UI-saliency como UEyes/Imp1k/WebSaliency nunca surgiram); **se DeepGaze ainda é SOTA em 2026 é em si não-verificado**. | [DeepGaze IIE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2105.12441) · [PerViT](https://proceedings.neurips.cc/paper_files/paper/2022/hash/cf78a15772ec1a6aee9bbee2d2b382c3-Abstract-Conference.html) · [V*/SEAL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.14135) |
> 114	| O análogo de sistemas — estágio barato de alto-recall rejeitando a maioria, reservando o estágio caro/preciso para casos ambíguos (linhagem **Viola-Jones**; probes antes de classificadores LLM caros) — é padrão de eficiência de produção. | ✅ Confirmado (cascatas reportam ~3× redução de latência) — valida a arquitetura proposta independentemente de visão. ⚠️ Figuras de speedup de robótica foveada (94%/7×/3×) **não-verificadas**. | [Cascaded pipelines](https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/cascaded-detection-pipelines) |
> 115	
> 116	---
> 117	
> 118	## 3. Arquitetura "Essência Humana"
> 119	
> 120	### O design unificador: percepção em dois níveis
> 121	
> 122	```
> 123	                          ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
> 124	                          │  TIER 1 — PERIFERIA (barata, always-on, alto-recall)  │
> 125	   input (imagem/         │  "dorsal / where-how": princípios neuromórficos        │
> 126	    vídeo/screen/         │   EMULADOS EM SOFTWARE                                  │
> 127	    stream/URL)  ───────▶ │   • frame-diff / MOG2 (OpenCV, CPU)                     │
> 128	                          │   • síntese de eventos v2e/OpenEB → voxel-grid          │
> 129	                          │   • saliência DeepGaze (cheap "where to look")          │
> 130	                          │   • pop-out de feature única (FIT, paralelo)            │
> 131	                          └───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┘
> 132	                                          │  SALIENCY / EVENT TRIGGER
> 133	                                          │  (só dispara se há sinal — gate)
> 134	                                          ▼
> 135	                          ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
> 136	                          │  TIER 2 — FÓVEA (cara, profunda, sob-demanda)          │
> 137	                          │  "ventral / what": identidade & semântica              │
> 138	                          │   • crop foveal → VLM (Gemini/Qwen/Claude/GPT-5.x)     │
> 139	                          │   • SAM 2/3 segmentação promptável                     │
> 140	                          │   • OCR (Mistral/PaddleOCR)                            │
> 141	                          │   • Gemini video API (temporal aberto)                 │
> 142	                          └───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┘
> 143	                                          │  extração estruturada
> 144	                                          ▼
> 145	                          ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
> 146	                          │  DEEPSEARCH GROUNDING (Firecrawl, per-user)            │
> 147	                          │   query ▶ scrape/search ▶ "referências consolidadas"  │
> 148	                          └───────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┘
> 149	                                          ▼
> 150	                          ┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
> 151	                          │  KNOWLEDGE MESH (mcorch_nodes/edges, user+project)    │
> 152	                          │   nó observation + content_mesh_asset                 │
> 153	                          │   ▶ embed-mcorch-node → pgvector(768) (grátis)        │
> 154	                          │   ▶ provenance/license/retention metadata             │
> 155	                          └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
> 156	```
> 157	
> 158	**Por que dois níveis (fundamento + economia).** A biologia (Pilar IV) e o XR de produção provam que concentrar compute caro onde há sinal economiza 33–52% de GPU com perda de acurácia negligenciável (Quest Pro ETFR; modelo foveal-peripheral Frontiers 2021 ~10× mais rápido a 0.39% de perda). No nosso domínio econômico isso é literal: o **Tier 1 roda em CPU quase-grátis** (1 mco ou 0), e o **Tier 2 só queima mcoCoins quando o gate dispara** — a periferia neuromórfica emulada é o que mantém o custo por chamada baixo.
> 159	
> 160	**Honestidade do limite.** O Tier 1 NÃO entrega resolução temporal µs (eventos sintéticos são interpolados, não observados — Pilar III). E a saliência DeepGaze **ainda não tem transferência comprovada para telas/UIs** (Pilar IV, gap aberto). Portanto o gate periférico é especificado como **alto-recall com custo de falso-negativo explícito** (o que o Tier 1 não disparar, o Tier 2 nunca vê) — uma decisão de design que o FRD precisa calibrar com um alvo de recall medido (Seção 8).
> 161	
> 162	### Como o deepsearch fundamentado alimenta a Knowledge Mesh
> 163	
> 164	Cada extração de deepsearch ("referência de mercado consolidada") vira um nó na malha seguindo o shape `content_mesh_asset` (precedente `orchestrate-step/index.ts:445-462`), com `user_id + project_id` (isolamento tenant, migration `20260425210000`), e é **embeddada de graça** pelo pipeline `embed-mcorch-node` (OpenRouter text-embedding-3-small → fallback Gemini text-embedding-004, 768 dims). Isso casa o diferencial de produto (referências consolidadas) com o GraphRAG existente: cada referência ganha edges para o nó de query/run, fica buscável por `match_mcorch_nodes`, e é submetida ao decaimento FSRS/`stability_score`. **Regra de soberania de dados:** conteúdo scrapeado NUNCA entra em system nodes (`user_id IS NULL` — visível a todos os tenants) e carrega metadata de proveniência/licença/retenção (Seções 7–8).

> 221	
> 222	## 5. Arquitetura MCORCH-nativa
> 223	
> 224	> Cada escolha aterrada em referência concreta de repo que o agente de mapeamento estabeleceu (e que confirmei materialmente nesta sessão).
> 225	
> 226	### 5.1 Runtime recomendado — Container no Sovereign Mesh
> 227	
> 228	**Decisão: Docker container `mcorch_vision_mcp`** no Sovereign Mesh, **Node/Bun + `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` `StreamableHTTPServerTransport`**, publicado **loopback-only** (`127.0.0.1:<porta>`) e proxiado pelo nginx do host sob um novo vhost **`mcp.mcorch.com`**.
> 229	
> 230	**Por quê (3 razões aterradas):**
> 231	1. **Precedente direto.** `mcorch_gitnexus` já roda um MCP server in-house com `StreamableHTTPServerTransport` em `127.0.0.1:3100` dentro do mesh hardened — confirmado em `packages/gitnexus-core/src/mcp/server.ts:16,97` + `docker-compose.yml:89-119`. O envelope de hardening (`x-hardening` anchor em `docker-compose.yml:8`: `cap_drop`/`read_only`/UID 1001/`pids_limit`), o healthcheck node (`docker-compose.yml:113`) e a telemetria watchdog são reutilizáveis as-is. `mcorch_vision_mcp` entra na rede `mcorch-sovereign-net`.
> 232	2. **Timeouts matam a rota Edge.** O histórico do próprio repo prova que limites de wall-clock do Supabase Edge forçaram o pipeline orchestrate a virar passos async encadeados por pg_net (`docs/processes/orchestrate-async-pipeline.md`; RPC `async_orchestrate_step`). Deepsearch é, por definição, trabalho de minutos — não pode bloquear num isolate Edge. Um container long-lived não tem teto de execução (nginx `proxy_read_timeout` + `proxy_buffering off` tratam SSE) e zero cold-start.
> 233	3. **systemd (opção c) regrediria o BoK de segurança.** O FM-10 foi corrigido movendo o gitnexus para um container hardened não-root (`docker-compose.yml:2-3`); um serviço tenant-facing/billable merece o mesmo envelope `cap_drop`/`no-new-privileges`/`mem_limit`, não um processo bare no host.
> 234	
> 235	**Caveat material:** NÃO adicionar campo `name:` ao `docker-compose.yml` (o projeto deve permanecer `constellation-orchestra` ou containers rodando viram órfãos). Para deepsearch: implementar **semântica de job dentro do container** (tool `start` retorna `job_id` + progress notifications / tool `poll`), não chamada bloqueante.
> 236	
> 237	### 5.2 Auth — API Tenancy per-user (fail-closed)
> 238	
> 239	O container **não** fica atrás do Kong, então a verificação de identidade é 100% in-app. O repo já resolveu exatamente isso para edge functions `verify_jwt=false`: **verificação ES256 via JWKS** (`docs/processes/edge-jwt-identity-verification.md:47-79` — `jose` `jwtVerify` + `createRemoteJWKSet` contra `…/auth/v1/.well-known/jwks.json`). O mesmo código roda em Node.
> 240	
> 241	- **Ordem de resolução de credencial (mandatória, CLAUDE.md §API Tenancy):** (1) tabela per-user (`decrypted_user_api_keys` por `user_id`, padrão `orchestrate-content/index.ts:60-66`) → (2) env vault fallback só para cron/service-role → (3) hard-fail HTTP 402/501 estruturado.
> 242	- **Gap conhecido:** **não existe coluna `firecrawl_api_key`** em `user_api_keys` hoje (firecrawl só aparece em scripts QA com env key). Adicionar a coluna + UI Settings + fail-closed `firecrawl_not_configured` (padrão de 1 linha: migration `20260601060000_user_api_keys_apify_token.sql:12`; encryption pelo Vault trigger family).
> 243	- **Anti-pattern proibido:** `Deno.env.get('FIRECRAWL_API_KEY')` em fluxo user-facing sem fallback per-user → exaustão de quota por um tenant, sem atribuição de receita, blast radius financeiro compartilhado.
> 244	- **Regra anti-confused-deputy:** o `deduct_mco_coins` tem grant service_role (`20260603220000`); o container só pode deduzir contra `user_id` **verificado por ele mesmo** (sub do token assinado), nunca de campo não-verificado. NUNCA `atob` cego no sub (`edge-jwt-identity-verification.md:118-124`).
> 245	
> 246	### 5.3 Billing — mcoCoins atômico (modelo 4×-floor)
> 247	
> 248	- **Dedução atômica:** RPC `deduct_mco_coins(p_user_id, p_amount, p_action, p_context)` (assinatura `20260603220000:37-74`), chamada via PostgREST com `SB_SECRET_KEY` **após** verificar identidade. Saldo insuficiente → **HTTP 402**.
> 249	- **Cobrança na entrada + refund-on-failure:** `refundMco` (`_shared/billing.ts:20-38`, credit service-role-only, nunca lança); padrão catch `orchestrate-content/index.ts:207-221`.
> 250	- **Metering por leg LLM:** `_shared/agent-metering.ts:69-127` (`calc_agent_cost_mcoin` + ledger `agent_executions` + `deduct_mco_coins`); **BYOK anda de graça** (`isUserCustomKey → cost 0`); exempção `SOVEREIGN_USER_ID`.
> 251	- **Fórmula de preço (SSOT `docs/processes/mcoin-cost-calibration.md`, confirmada):**
> 252	  `mco(op) = ceil( real_cost_usd(op) / USD_PER_MCO_FLOOR ($0.018) × MARGIN (4) )`.
> 253	  **Classes de preço (derivadas dos custos medidos ao vivo 2026-06-11 — Seção 6).** Regra do SOP: medir o $ real materialmente antes de fixar; espelhar em `src/lib/billing.ts → COIN_COSTS` **e** numa constante hardcoded no serviço (ele não importa `src/lib`); asserir em `src/test/billing.test.ts`.
> 254	
> 255	### 5.4 Mesh Connection Mandate
> 256	
> 257	Template `orchestrate-step/index.ts:424-462`: em todo run bem-sucedido, inserir nó `observation` em `mcorch_nodes` (`metadata.cost_mco`, `stability_score 1.0`) + `content_mesh_asset` com `project_id`. Toda falha → `infra_health_logs` com `service='vision-mcp'` (padrão fire-and-forget `_shared/llm-fallback.ts:19-35`). Traceabilidade: cada nó/log carrega o BR/FR ID da suíte BoK (Seção 10). Health do container → Neural HUD via `scripts/watchdog-mcorch.sh` + `get-infra-status` (zero novo encanamento depois que o watchdog aprende o nome do container).
> 258	
> 259	### 5.5 Exposição externa — Streamable HTTP + OAuth 2.1
> 260	
> 261	Conformidade mínima 2025-11-25 (servidor remoto protegido):
> 262	- **Transporte:** endpoint único Streamable HTTP (POST → `application/json` ou `text/event-stream`; GET → SSE server-initiated). Validar header **Origin** (403 em inválido, anti DNS-rebinding). Honrar `MCP-Protocol-Version` por request. Usar a semântica de disconnect/poll do SSE para sobreviver a janelas de wall-clock e limites do Cloudflare ([transports](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/transports)).
> 263	- **Auth:** RFC 9728 Protected Resource Metadata (`/.well-known/oauth-protected-resource`) apontando ao AS; bearer no header **Authorization** em todo request (nunca query string); validar **audience** do token; **token passthrough proibido**; PKCE S256; preferir **Client ID Metadata Documents** sobre DCR ([authorization](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25/basic/authorization)). O AS pode ser o Supabase Auth fronteado, ou um vendor (WorkOS/Auth0) — decisão do SDD.
> 264	- **PAT para clients MCP externos:** sessions JWT do Supabase expiram em ~1h, inúteis para configs de client long-lived. **Não existe** tabela de PAT no repo. **Novo design obrigatório:** tabela `mcp_access_tokens` (`user_id`, **hash SHA-256 do token** — nunca plaintext/Vault-reversível, `scopes`, `expires_at`, `revoked_at`, RLS `auth.uid()=user_id`, default-deny) + `/security-review` na migration (mandato FMEA-011). Scopes DEVEM excluir gestão de credencial (vazamento de PAT = dreno direto da carteira mcoCoins).
> 265	- **Descoberta:** publicar `server.json` via `mcp-publisher` sob namespace verificado `com.mcorch/*` (challenge DNS/HTTP) no MCP Registry (preview) ([registry](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/registry/about)).
> 266	- **Sentinel:** `_shared/sentinel.ts` (TS puro, portável a Node) — `inspectPrompt` L1/L2/L3 **após** identidade, **antes** de cobrar; `logSentinelBlock → infra_health_logs service='sentinel'`.
> 267	
> 268	---
> 269	
> 270	## 6. Superfície MCP proposta
> 271	
> 272	> Rascunho de tools/resources/prompts. Custo em classes mcoCoins derivadas dos preços medidos ao vivo (2026-06-11), fórmula `ceil(usd/$0.018×4)`. **Toda tool: gate sentinel `inspectPrompt` nos args + verificação de identidade antes de cobrar.** Números são **calibração inicial** — o SOP exige medição material antes do fix em `COIN_COSTS`.
> 273	
> 274	### Tools (model-controlled — front-door para ecossistemas externos)
> 275	
> 276	| Tool | Contrato (1 linha) | Input gate (sentinel) | Classe mcoCoin |
> 277	|---|---|---|---|
> 278	| `vision.describe_image` | VLM QA sobre 1 imagem (Gemini/Qwen/Claude/GPT-5.x) → texto/structuredContent | inspect prompt + URL/host allowlist | **2** (cobre lane premium: img única $0.0006–0.0107) |
> 279	| `vision.detect_objects` | Detecção de objetos via Gemini native boxes `[ymin,xmin,ymax,xmax]` | inspect prompt | **2** |
> 280	| `vision.segment` | Segmentação promptável (texto/box/point) via SAM-3 (fal) / SAM-2 (Replicate) | inspect prompt | **2** (fal $0.005) / **5** (Replicate $0.019) |
> 281	| `vision.ocr` | OCR documento → markdown/JSON (Mistral OCR 3 direta / PaddleOCR CPU) | inspect + provenance tag | **1 / página** ($0.002→margem 9×) |
> 282	| `vision.analyze_video` | QA temporal sobre vídeo (Gemini video, 1 FPS default) | inspect + duração cap | **2 / minuto** (default res; low-res ~0.7/min) |
> 283	| `motion.peripheral_scan` | **Tier 1** — gate barato: frame-diff/MOG2 + saliência → regiões salientes (retorna `resource_link` p/ crops) | inspect | **1** ou **0** (CPU) |
> 284	| `motion.track` | Pose (RTMPose/MediaPipe) + tracking (ByteTrack/BoT-SORT) sobre vídeo curto | inspect | **1** (hot GPU ≤8s L40S) |
> 285	| `deepsearch.scrape` | Scrape de 1 URL pública (Firecrawl, logged-out) → markdown + provenance | inspect + robots.txt + login-wall block | **1** ($0.00083/página) |
> 286	| `deepsearch.run` | Job de deepsearch (N searches + scrapes) → `job_id`; conclui via progress/poll | inspect + allowlist de fontes | **3** (bundle ~$0.013) |
> 287	| `deepsearch.poll` | Recupera resultado de job assíncrono por `job_id` | scope check | **0** |
> 288	| `mesh.search` | Busca semântica na Knowledge Mesh do tenant (`match_mcorch_nodes`) | scope `auth.uid()` | **0–1** |
> 289	| `mesh.consolidate_reference` | Grava referência consolidada (vision+deepsearch) como nó observation + embed | inspect + LGPD/PII strip + provenance | **1** (embed grátis) |
> 290	
> 291	### Resources (application-driven — progressive enhancement p/ hosts completos)
> 292	
> 293	| Resource (URI template) | Conteúdo |
> 294	|---|---|
> 295	| `mesh://nodes/{node_id}` | Nó da malha (referência/observation) do tenant, RLS-scoped |
> 296	| `mesh://references?pillar={cv\|motion\|neuromorphic\|foveated}` | Corpus de referências consolidadas por pilar |
> 297	| `vision://artifacts/{content_ref}` | Frames/crops cacheados (Storage privado, signed URL curto) |
> 298	
> 299	> ⚠️ O Claude API MCP connector consome **só tools** — resources/prompts são enhancement para hosts completos (Claude Desktop/Code, VS Code). Toda capability que precisa ser alcançável por ecossistemas externos precisa de uma **porta tool**.
> 300	
> 301	### Prompts (user-controlled — workflows empacotados, estilo skills/slash)
> 302	
> 303	| Prompt | Workflow |
> ```

> 312	
> 313	## 7. Riscos & FMEA-seed
> 314	
> 315	| # | Vetor | Modo de falha | Severidade | Mitigação (gate) |
> 316	|---|---|---|---|---|
> 317	| FM-VM-01 | **Security — superfície pública** | `mcp.mcorch.com` é o 1º serviço tenant-facing fora do gateway Supabase; bug fail-open = impersonação cross-tenant (a classe que o SOP ES256 corrigiu) | Crítica | JWKS-verify antes de confiar no `sub`; **fail-closed** se JWKS inalcançável; nunca expor `SB_SECRET_KEY` em path client-reachable |
> 318	| FM-VM-02 | **Prompt-injection (inbound)** | Args de tool hostis manipulam o agente | Alta | `_shared/sentinel.ts` `inspectPrompt` L1/L2/L3 nos args, antes de cobrar |
> 319	| FM-VM-03 | **Indirect prompt-injection (scraped)** | Conteúdo web hostil scrapeado vira nó da malha consumido por agentes internos (corpus Pantheon) — vetor de injeção **persistente** | Alta | Rodar sentinel L2 (`lexicalScore`/control-chars) sobre texto **extraído** antes do insert; tag de origem `scraped` no metadata p/ trust por proveniência |
> 320	| FM-VM-04 | **Image-borne injection** | Instruções do atacante renderizadas **dentro** da imagem/tela, lavadas via OCR/VLM para o loop do agente | Média-Alta | ⚠️ Não pesquisado a fundo — sentinel cobre texto; **estender SOP a outputs de vision-tool** (citar OWASP LLM01 / MCP tool-poisoning) |
> 321	| FM-VM-05 | **Tenancy — firecrawl env key** | Shared `FIRECRAWL_API_KEY` em fluxo user-facing → exaustão de quota, sem atribuição de receita, blast radius compartilhado | Alta | Coluna `firecrawl_api_key` per-user + UI + 402 `firecrawl_not_configured` antes de exposição externa |
> 322	| FM-VM-06 | **Billing — PAT leak** | Vazamento de PAT = dreno direto da carteira mcoCoins via tool calls billable | Crítica | `mcp_access_tokens` com hash SHA-256, scopes (sem gestão de credencial), `expires_at`/`revoked_at`, RLS default-deny, `/security-review` |
> 323	| FM-VM-07 | **Billing — job parcial** | Job de minutos que sucede parcialmente (5 de 12 páginas) tem semântica de refund ambígua | Média | Definir regra de **partial-delivery refund** no FRD antes do código; cobrança contra identidade verificada pelo container |
> 324	| FM-VM-08 | **Licensing — AGPL/bespoke** | Ultralytics AGPL servido em rede sem licença Enterprise = violação; SAM/DINOv3 propagam termos de redistribuição | Alta | SDD escolhe pista Apache (SAM 2, RF-DETR, Qwen3-VL, OpenEB); se SAM 3/DINOv3, importar atribuição + propagação + gated-distribution; fetch dos LICENSE files |
> 325	| FM-VM-09 | **LGPD — dado biométrico** | Faces/gait são **dado sensível** (LGPD art. 5º II); só consentimento específico-destacado (art. 11 I) ou lista fechada art. 11 II — **legítimo interesse NÃO se aplica**; ANPD lista "modo de andar" como biométrico | Crítica | Escopar vision **para detecção/descrição, NÃO geração de template/recognition/classificação** sem base art. 11 documentada; watch da regulação ANPD 2026 |
> 326	| FM-VM-10 | **LGPD — PII de terceiros / transferência internacional** | Screenshots/scraping capturam PII; envio a APIs US (OpenAI/Google/OpenRouter) é transferência internacional; **não há adequação p/ EUA** (só UE via Res. 32/2026) | Alta | PII-strip antes do insert; SCCs ANPD (Res. 19/2024) no DPA do provider, ou roteamento p/ território adequado, ou consentimento art. 33 VIII destacado |
> 327	| FM-VM-11 | **LGPD/IP — erasure & corpus** | Nós persistentes/embeddados/bridgeados p/ memória OpenClaw sem path de apagamento; precedente hiQ×LinkedIn alcançou o **corpus derivado** ($500k + deleção) | Alta | RPC `erase_vision_artifacts(content_ref)` espelhando `erase_lead()` (cascade: nó + edges + embedding + frames Storage + cópias provider); retention clock (art. 16); allowlist de fontes permissivas (Wikipedia/PLOS/Frontiers/SEC EDGAR/IPFS); honrar robots.txt + CDSM Art. 4 opt-out |
> 328	| FM-VM-12 | **Custo — cold start** | Inferência GPU sub-segundo a 1 mco só fecha com container **warm**; cold start (10–60s) estoura o budget | Média | Keep-warm ou batch semantics; medir $ real antes de fixar mco |
> 329	| FM-VM-13 | **Process — BoK gate** | Código escrito sem `docs/bok/vision-mcp/{01-mrd..05-sdd}` viola Closed-Loop Protocol; sem BR/FR IDs não há traceabilidade de mesh-mandate | Bloqueante | `/bok-scribe vision-mcp` antes de qualquer código; Lei 2 — SOP `docs/processes/vision-mcp-*.md` antes da automação |
